         &\left|\frac{1}{X}\sum_{x\in I}e^{if(x)}\right|^{2^k}\\
         \ll&  \frac{1}{X} + \frac{1}{XH_1\cdots H_k}\sum_{\mathbf{h}\in H_{\times}}\left( \alpha \lambda^{1/2}(h_1\cdots h_k)^{1/2}X+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}(h_1\cdots h_k)^{1/2}}\right)\label{eq:almost_there}.
    \end{align}
    Next, we bound the sum over $\mathbf{h}$ by noting that $\sum_{h_i=1}^{H_i}h_i^{1/2} \leqslant H_i^{3/2}\leqslant \frac{X^{3/2}}{(2k)^{3/2}}$ and $\sum_{h_i=1}^{H_i}h_i^{-1/2} \leqslant 2H_i^{1/2}\leqslant \frac{2X^{1/2}}{(2k)^{1/2}}$ for each $i$. So,
    \begin{align*}
        \sum_{\mathbf{h}\in H_{\times}} \alpha \lambda^{1/2}(h_1\cdots h_k)^{1/2}X+\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}(h_1\cdots h_k)^{1/2}} \ll \alpha\lambda^{1/2}X^{3k/2+1} +\frac{X^{k/2}}{\lambda^{1/2}}.
    \end{align*}
    Noting that $H_1\cdots H_k\gg X^k$, we combine this with (\ref{eq:almost_there}) to obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \left|\frac{1}{X}\sum_{x\in I}e^{if(x)}\right|^{2^k}
         \ll&  \frac{1}{X} +  \frac{1}{X^{k+1}}\left(\alpha\lambda^{1/2}X^{3k/2+1}+\frac{X^{k/2}}{\lambda^{1/2}}\right)\\
         =&\frac{1}{X} +\alpha(\lambda X^{k})^{1/2}+\frac{1}{(\lambda X^{k+2})^{1/2}}
         \\=&\frac{1}{X} +\alpha(\lambda X^{j-2})^{1/2}+\frac{1}{(\lambda X^{j})^{1/2}}.
    \end{align*}
    Taking $2^k$th roots of both sides gives (\ref{eq:better_vdc}), which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Fix a nonzero $k\in \mathbb{Z}$. We will prove the statement of the theorem by induction on $d\in \mathbb{N}$.

The base case $d=1$ is true by Theorem \ref{thm:d=1_thm} and Theorem \ref{thm:mikey_thm}. More specifically, let $d=1$ and find a function $V$ which belongs a maximal Hardy field containing $f$ and satisfies $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{(f^{(d)}(x))^{1/d}}{(\log V)'(x)}=1$ (see (\ref{eq:maximal_hardy_field_function})). From Theorem \ref{thm:d=1_thm}, we have that $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^We^{2\pi i kf(n)}=0$ for each $W$ which belongs to a Hardy field containing $f$ and $V$, which tends to $\infty$ and satisfies $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{\log W(x)}{\log V(x)} = 0$. Then the implication $(2)\implies (3)$ in Theorem \ref{thm:mikey_thm} shows that (\ref{eq:positive_results_goal}) holds for any $s:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow \mathbb{N}$ which satisfies $\lim_{N\to\infty}s(N)\cdot (\log V)'(N) = \lim_{N\to\infty}s(N)\cdot (f^{(d)}(x))^{1/d} = \infty$, and this shows that the base case holds.


Now for the induction step. 
Let $d\geqslant 2$ and suppose that for any Hardy function $F$ with $x^{d-2}\prec F(x)\prec x^{d-1}$ and for any $s:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow \mathbb{N}$ with 
    $\lim_{N\to\infty}s(N)^{d-1}\cdot F^{(d-1)}(N)=\infty$ and $s(N)\leqslant N-1$ for all sufficiently large $N\in\mathbb{N}$, we have 
    \begin{equation*}
    \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{s(N)}\sum_{n=N-s(N)}^Ne^{2\pi i kF(n)}=0
\end{equation*}
    for all nonzero $k\in \mathbb{Z}$.

Fix $s:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow \mathbb{N}$ with $s(N)\leqslant N-1$ for all sufficiently large $N\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $f$ be a Hardy function with $x^{d-1}\prec f(x)\prec x^d$ and $\lim_{N\to\infty}s(N)^d\cdot f^{(d)}(N)=\infty$. There are two cases to consider: the case where $\lim_{x\to\infty}s(x)^{d}\cdot f^{(d+1)}(x)=\infty$ and the case where $\lim_{x\to\infty}s(x)^{d}\cdot f^{(d+1)}(x)<\infty$.


First suppose that $\lim_{x\to\infty}s(x)^{d}\cdot f^{(d+1)}(x)=\infty$. Let $j\in \mathbb{N}$ and apply the induction hypothesis to $F(n)=f(n+j)-f(n)$, which satisfies $F(x) = j\cdot f^{'}(x)\cdot (1+o_{x\to\infty}(1))$ and hence $\lim_{x\to\infty}s(x)^{d}\cdot f^{(d)}(x)=\infty$, so that 
$$
\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{s(N)}\sum_{n=N-s(N)}^Ne^{2\pi i k (f(n+j)-f(n))}=0
$$
for all $k\in \mathbb{N}$ and hence (\ref{eq:positive_results_goal}) holds for all $k\in \mathbb{N}$ by applying Theorem \ref{thm:vdc_trick} with $I_{N} = [N-s(N),N]$ for $N\in \mathbb{N}$.

    On the other hand, suppose that  $\lim_{x\to\infty}f^{(d+1)}(x)\cdot s(x)^{d}<\infty$. Then it must be that $\lim_{x\to\infty}f^{(d+1)}(x)\cdot {s}(x)^{d-1}=0$. Take $j=d+1$, $X=s(N)$, $I = [N-s(N),N]$, $\lambda = f^{(d+1)}(N)$, $\alpha = \frac{f^{(d+1)}(N-s(N))}{f^{(d+1)}(N)}$ in order to apply Lemma \ref{lem:second_derivative_vdc_inequality} if $d=1$ or Theorem \ref{thm:better_vdc} if $d\geqslant 2$. We have $X\to \infty$, $X\lambda^{j-2}\to 0$, and $X\lambda^j \to \infty$ as $N\to\infty$ by assumption, and using the argument in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:shift_inequality} we see that $\alpha\to 1$ as $N\to\infty$. In the case $d=1$ or $d>1$, we can use (\ref{eq:second_derivative_vdc_inequality}) or (\ref{eq:better_vdc}), respectively, to see that $$
    \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{s(N)}\sum_{n=N-s(N)}^Ne^{2\pi i f(n)}=0.
    $$
    This completes the proof of the induction step and so we are done.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{def:nilsystem}
  Let $G$ be a nilpotent Lie group. 
  We say that a closed subgroup $\Gamma\subseteq G$ is \emph{uniform} if $X = G/\Gamma$ is compact, and we say that $\Gamma$ is \emph{discrete} if there is an open cover of $\Gamma$ in which each element of $\Gamma$ belongs to a unique element of the cover. When $\Gamma$ is uniform and discrete, $X$ is called a \emph{nilmanifold}. We will use $\mu_X$ to denote the Haar measure on a nilmanifold $X$. A measure preserving system of the form $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$, where $X$ is a nilmanifold and $T(x\Gamma) = ax\Gamma$ for some $a\in G$, is called a \emph{nilsystem}.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{def:u.d.}
 Let $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence in a nilmanifold $X$ with measure $\mu$. We say that $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is \emph{uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages} in $(X,\mu)$ if 
 \begin{equation}
          \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^W_{n\leqslant N}F(x_n) = \int_X F~d\mu
 \end{equation}
 for all $F\in C(X)$ (we may say $X$ instead $(X,\mu)$ if the measure is the Haar measure $\mu_X$). When $W(N)=N$, we call $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ \emph{uniformly distributed} in $(X,\mu)$. Let $(Y_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence of subnilmanifolds of a nilmanifold $X$. We say that $(Y_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed in $X$ if 
  \begin{equation}
          \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}F(Y_n) = \int_X F~d\mu_X
 \end{equation}
 for all $F\in C(X)$. Here and for the rest of the paper, we use the notation $F(Y) =\int_Y F~d\mu_Y$ for a subnilmanifold $Y$ and a function $F\in C(X)$.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
\label{def:relatively_independent_product}
Let $G$ be a nilpotent Lie group, let $\Gamma\subseteq G$ be a uniform and discrete subgroup, and let $X=G/\Gamma$. Let $L\subseteq G$ be a normal subgroup which is also \emph{rational}, meaning that the set of rational elements $\{x\in L: x^n\in \Gamma\text{ for some } n\in \mathbb{N}\}$ is dense in $L$. Define
\begin{align*}
    &G\times_L G = \{(g_1,g_2)\in G\times G: g_1g_2^{-1}\in L\},\\
    &\Gamma\times_L \Gamma =\{(g_1,g_2)\in \Gamma\times \Gamma: g_1g_2^{-1}\in L\}= (\Gamma\times \Gamma)\cap (G\times_L G), \\
    &X\times_L X = (G\times_L G)/(\Gamma \times_L \Gamma).
\end{align*}
(Observe that $G \times_L G$ is a nilpotent Lie group and $X \times_L X$ is a nilmanifold.)
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:wd_in_diagonal}
Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group, $\Gamma$ a uniform and discrete subgroup of $G$ and consider the nilmanifold $X=G/\Gamma$. Let $b \in G$ be arbitrary and let $L$ denote the smallest connected, normal, rational, and closed subgroup of $G$ containing $b^{\mathbb{R}} = \{b^t : t \in \mathbb{R}\}$. Let $X^{\triangle}$ denote the diagonal $\{(x, x) : x \in X\}$. Then for all but countably many $\xi \in\mathbb{R}$, the sequence of subnilmanifolds $((b^{\xi n}, 1_G)X^{\triangle}))_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed in the
relatively independent product $X\times_L X$, where we identify $(g\Gamma, g\Gamma)$ with $(g,g)\Gamma\times_L \Gamma$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
Let $G$ be a nilpotent Lie group, let $\Gamma$ be a uniform and discrete subgroup of $G$, and let $X=G/\Gamma$. A pair $(\varphi,\chi)$ is called a \emph{central character} of $(G,\Gamma)$ if $\varphi:X\rightarrow  \mathbb{C}$ is continuous and $\chi:Z(G)\rightarrow \mathbb{T}\subseteq \mathbb{C}$ is a continuous group homomorphism defined on the center of $G$, such that $\varphi(yx) = \chi(y)\varphi(x)$ for all $y\in Z(G)$ and all $x\in X$.
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:richter_equivalence}
Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent
Lie group, $\Gamma$ a uniform and discrete subgroup of $G$, and $X=G/\Gamma$. 
Let $v:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow G$ be a sequence in $G$ and let $L$ be a rational, closed, normal subgroup of $G$ such that $L^{\circ}\cap Z(G)^{\circ}$ is nontrivial.
Then the following are equivalent:
    \begin{itemize}
   \item The sequence $(v(n)\Gamma)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $X$.
   \item For each central character $(\varphi,\chi)$ such that $\chi$ is nontrivial on $L^{\circ}\cap Z(G)^{\circ}$
   \begin{equation}\label{eq:richter_equivalence}
        \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\varphi(v(n)\Gamma)=0.
    \end{equation}
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
Let $G$ be a nilpotent Lie group, let $\Gamma$ be a discrete and uniform subgroup of $G$, and let $X=G/\Gamma$. A \emph{horizontal character} for $(G,\Gamma)$ is a continuous function $\eta:X\rightarrow \mathbb{T}\subseteq \mathbb{C}$ such that 
    $$
    \eta(ab\Gamma) = \eta(a\Gamma)\eta(b\Gamma)
    $$
    for all $a,b\in G$.
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:vdc}
Let $W$ be a function which tends to $\infty$ such that $\Delta W$ is eventually monotone. Additionally, assume that $\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\Delta W(N)}{W(N)}=0$. Let $(u_{n,N})_{n,N\in \mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence in a Hilbert space such that $|u_{n,N}|\leqslant 1$ for all $n,N\in \mathbb{N}$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:vdc_eq}
    \limsup_{N\to\infty}\|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wu_{n,N}\|^2 \leqslant \limsup_{H\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\langle u_{n+h,N},u_{n,N}\rangle\right|.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{remark:vdc}
    We can bound the right hand side of (\ref{eq:vdc_eq}) using the triangle inequality and linearity to see that
\begin{align*}\label{eq:vdc_eq_2}
\limsup_{H\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty}&\left|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\langle u_{n+h,N},u_{n,N}\rangle\right|\\\leqslant& \limsup_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}\left(\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\langle u_{n+h,N},u_{n,N}\rangle\right|\right).
\end{align*}
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- theorem-17: body_md -->
\label{thm:stolz_cesaro}
Let $(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ and $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ be real valued sequences such that $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ increases to $\infty$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:stolz_cesaro_derivative}
    \liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Delta a_n}{\Delta b_n}\leqslant \liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{a_n}{b_n}\leqslant \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{a_n}{b_n}\leqslant \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Delta a_n}{\Delta b_n}.
\end{equation}
In particular, if $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Delta a_n}{\Delta b_n}$ exists then $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{ a_n}{ b_n}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Delta a_n}{\Delta b_n}$.
<!-- end theorem-17 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:wd_means_W_ud}
    Let $Y$ be a Banach space and let $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}\subseteq Y$ be a bounded sequence such that the limit $
        \lim_{N-M\to\infty}\frac{1}{N-M}\sum_{n=M}^Nx_n$ exists and is equal to $L\in Y$. Let $(\alpha_{N,n})_{N,n\in \mathbb{N}}$ define a regular matrix method (see Lemma \ref{lem:silverman_toeplitz}) with the additional property that for any $k\in \mathbb{Z}$ and any bounded sequence $(y_{n})_{n\in \mathbb{N}},$
        $$
        \sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{N,n}y_{n+k} = \sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{N,n}y_n +o_{N\to\infty}(1).
        $$
        Then $\lim_{N\to\infty} \sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{N,n}x_{n}=L$. In particular, $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wx_n = L$ for any $W$ which eventually increases to $\infty$ and satisfies $\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\Delta W(N)}{W(N)}=0$.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let $\varepsilon>0$. Pick $K\in \mathbb{N}$ large enough so that if $N-M\geqslant K$ then 
    $$
    \left|L-\frac{1}{N-M}\sum_{k=M}^Nx_k\right|<\varepsilon.
    $$
    Let $y_n = L-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^Kx_{n+k}$ so that $|y_n|<\varepsilon$ for each $n\in \mathbb{N}$. Then $\left|\sum_{n=1}^N\alpha_{N,n}y_n\right|<\varepsilon+o_{N\to\infty}(1)$. By linearity and shift invariance, we also have 
    $$
    \sum_{n=1}^N\alpha_{N,n}y_n= L - \sum_{n=1}^N\alpha_{N,n}x_n  +o_{N\to\infty}(1).
    $$
    Hence $\left| L - \sum_{n=1}^N\alpha_{N,n}x_n\right|<\varepsilon+o_{N\to\infty}(1)$ and since $\varepsilon$ is arbitrary, we are done.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
The proof of Lemma \ref{lem:wd_means_W_ud} given here is similar to the argument given in \cite[Theorem 3.6]{tempered_functions}, which shows the implication $\lim_{N-M\to\infty}\sum_{n=M}^Nx_n = L \implies \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wx_n = L$ for functions satisfying $W$ such that $\Delta W$ eventually increases to $\infty$ and $\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\Delta W(N)}{W(N)}=0$.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{remark:wd_means_W_ud}
    The same argument used to prove Lemma \ref{lem:wd_means_W_ud} shows that if $d\in \mathbb{N}$ and $(x_{\mathbf{n}})_{\mathbf{n}\in \mathbb{N}^{d}}$ is a bounded sequence such that 
    \begin{equation}
    \frac{1}{(N_1-M_1)\cdots (N_d-M_d)}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in [M_1,N_1]\times\cdots \times [M_d,N_d]}x_{\mathbf{n}}
    \end{equation}
    converges to $L$ as $N_i-M_i\to\infty$ for all $i$, then 
    $$
    \lim_{N_1,\dots, N_d\to\infty} \sum_{n_1,\dots, n_d\in \mathbb{N}}\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d}x_{\mathbf{n}}=L,
    $$
    where $(\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d})_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d\in \mathbb{N}}$ is a matrix of real numbers satisfying 
    \begin{itemize}
        \item For each $i$, if the values of $n_1,\dots, n_d$ and $N_j$ for $j\neq i$ are fixed, then 
        $$
        \lim_{N_i \to \infty}\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d} = 0,
        $$
        \item $\lim_{N_1,\dots, N_d\to\infty} \sum_{n_1,\dots, n_d\in \mathbb{N}}\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d}=1$,
        \item $\lim_{N_1,\dots, N_d\to\infty} \sum_{n_1,\dots, n_d\in \mathbb{N}}|\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d}|<\infty$,
        \item for any $\mathbf{k}\in \mathbb{N}^d$ and any bounded sequence $(y_{\mathbf{n}})_{\mathbf{n}\in \mathbb{N}^d}$
        $$
        \lim_{N_1,\dots, N_d\to\infty}\left|\sum_{n_1,\dots, n_d\in \mathbb{N}}\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d}y_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{k}} -\sum_{n_1,\dots, n_d\in \mathbb{N}}\alpha_{N_1,\dots, N_d,n_1,\dots, n_d}y_{\mathbf{n}} \right|=0.
        $$
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
\label{def:uniformity_seminorms}
    For a measure preserving system $(X,\mathscr{B},\mu,T)$, the \emph{uniformity seminorms} on $L^{\infty}(X)$ are defined inductively by 
    \begin{equation*}
        \|h\|_0 = \int_X h~d\mu_X\quad \text{ and }\quad \|h\|_{s}^{2^s} = \operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}\|\overline{h}\cdot T^nh\|_{s-1}^{2^{s-1}}
    \end{equation*}
    for $h\in L^{\infty}(X)$ and $s\in \mathbb{N}$.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- theorem-18: body_md -->
\label{thm:generalized_BMR_4.2}
Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and $W \in \mathcal{H}$ with
$1 \prec \log W(x) \prec x$.
Let $\mathscr{F} = \{f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell}\}$ be a collection of functions such that $ f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell}\in \mathcal{H}$, $\mathscr{F}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) (Definition \ref{def:W_compatible}), $|f_1(x)| \preceq \cdots \preceq |f_{\ell}(x)|$, and $
\lim_{x\to\infty} |f_{\ell}(x)|=\lim_{x\to\infty} |f_{\ell}(x)-f_i(x)|
=
\infty
\quad \text{for every } i<\ell$. Then there exists $s\in\mathbb{N}$ such that for any invertible
measure preserving system $(X,\mathcal{B},\mu,T)$ and any $h_{\ell}\in L^\infty(X)$ with $\|h_{\ell}\|_s=0$ we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:4.2_goal}
\sup_{h_1,\ldots,h_{\ell-1}\in L^\infty}
\ \sup_{a\in \ell^\infty}
\left\|
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell} T^{[f_i(n)]}h_i
\right\|_{L^2}
=
o_{N\to\infty}(1),
\end{equation}
where the suprema are taken over all functions
$h_1,\ldots,h_{\ell-1}\in L^\infty(X)$ with
$\|h_i\|_{L^\infty}\le 1$
and all $a\in \ell^\infty(\mathbb{N})$ with
$\|a\|_{\ell^\infty}\le 1$.
<!-- end theorem-18 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
\label{def:property_P}
    Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and let $W,f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\in \mathcal{H}$. Suppose that $1\prec \log W(x)\prec x$, and that $f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}$ are each subpolynomial. We say that $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (P) if $W$ is compatible (Definition \ref{def:W_compatible}) with each $f\in \text{Span}\{f_i^{(m)}:1\leqslant i\leqslant \ell, m\geqslant 0\}$.
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{remark:P_WP_star}
    If $f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}$ each have degree $1$ then property (P) and property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) are equivalent.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- example-5: body_md -->
Recall from Definition \ref{def:W_compatible} that $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ is said to satisfy property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) if $W$ is compatible with each $f\in \text{Span}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$. Property (P) is more restrictive than property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) but this allows for sets satisfying Property (P) to be invariant under differentiation, meaning that if $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (P) then $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell},f_1',\dots, f_{\ell}'\}$ also satisfies property (P). The same is not true for property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}). For example, let $W(x)=x$ and let $f_1(x) = x\log (x)$ so that $f_1'(x) = \log(x)+1$. Then $\{f_1\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) but $\{f_1'\}$ does not.
<!-- end example-5 -->
<!-- theorem-19: body_md -->
\label{thm:BMR24_sublinear}
Let $k \in \mathbb{N}$.
Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and $W \in \mathcal{H}$ with
$1 \prec \log W(x)\prec x$.
Assume $f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell} \in \mathcal{H}$ satisfy property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}),
$|f_1(x)| \preceq \cdots \preceq |f_{\ell}(x)| \preceq x$, and $\lim_{x\to\infty} |f_{\ell}(x)|
=
\lim_{x\to\infty} |f_{\ell}(x)-f_i(x)|
=
\infty
\quad \text{for every } i<\ell$
Then there exists a constant $C_{\ell}>0$, depending only on
$\ell$ and $f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell}$, such that for any invertible
measure preserving system $(X,\mathcal{B},\mu,T)$ and any $h_{\ell}\in L^\infty(X)$ we have
\begin{equation}
\sup_{h_1,\ldots,h_{\ell-1}\in L^\infty}
\ \sup_{a\in \ell^\infty}
\left\|
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell} T^{[f_i(n)]}h_i
\right\|_{L^2}
\le
C_{\ell} \|h_{\ell}\|_{\ell+1}
+
o_{N\to\infty}(1),
\end{equation}
where the suprema are taken over all functions
$h_1,\ldots,h_{\ell-1}\in L^\infty(X)$ with
$\|h_i\|_{L^\infty}\le 1$
and all $a\in \ell^\infty(\mathbb{N})$ with
$\|a\|_{\ell^\infty}\le 1$.
<!-- end theorem-19 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Let $\mathscr{F} = \{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$. Without loss of generality we assume that $\lim_{x\to\infty}|f_i(x)|=\infty$ for each $i$, since if this is not true for some $i<\ell$ then we can factor $T^{\lim_{x\to\infty}[f_i(x)]}h_i+o_{N\to\infty}(1)$ out of the left-hand side of (\ref{eq:4.2_goal}).

We proceed by induction on the characteristic vector $(m_1(\mathscr{F}),\dots, m_{d_{\max}(\mathscr{F})}({\mathscr{F}}))$. 


For the base case of the induction, assume that $d_{\max}(\mathscr{F})=1$ and note that this case follows from Theorem \ref{thm:BMR24_sublinear} since if $d_{\max}(\mathscr{F})=1$ then the hypothesis of Theorem \ref{thm:BMR24_sublinear} holds and we can take $s=\ell+1$.



Now for the induction step. Suppose that $d_{\max}(\mathscr{F})\geqslant 2$ and that the statement of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_BMR_4.2} holds for all collections of functions $\mathscr{F}'$ with $$
(m_1(\mathscr{F}'),\dots, m_{d_{\max}(\mathscr{F}')}({\mathscr{F}}'))<(m_1(\mathscr{F}),\dots, m_{d_{\max}(\mathscr{F})}({\mathscr{F}})).
$$


We want to show that (\ref{eq:4.2_goal}) holds, so let $(X,\mathscr{B},\mu,T)$ be an invertible measure preserving system, fix a to be determined value of $s\in \mathbb{N}$, fix $a\in \ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})$ with $\|a\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1$, and let $(h_{i,N})_{N\in \mathbb{N}}$ be sequences of functions belonging to $L^{\infty}(X)$ for $i=1,\dots, \ell$ with $\|h_{i,N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant 1$ for all $i,N$, and $\|h_{\ell,N}\|_s=0$ for all $N\in \mathbb{N}$. 

Let $u_N(n)  = a(n)\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n) ]}h_{i,N}\in L^{\infty}(X)\subseteq L^2(X)$ for $n,N\in \mathbb{N}$. In light of Lemma \ref{lem:vdc} and Remark \ref{remark:vdc}, it suffices to show that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:vdc_goal}
   A(h):= \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\int u_N(n+m)\cdot \overline{u_N(n)}~d\mu\right)=0
\end{equation}
for each $h\in \mathbb{N}$ (since $L^2(X)$ is a Hilbert space with $\langle u_N(n+m),u_N(n)\rangle = \int u_N(n+m)\cdot \overline{u_N(n)}~d\mu $). So fix $h\in \mathbb{N}$ and observe that
\begin{align*}
   &\int u_N(n+h)\cdot \overline{u_N(n)} ~d\mu\\
   =&\int a(n+h)\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n+h) ]}h_{i,N}\cdot \overline{a(n)}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n) ]}\overline{h_{i,N}} ~d\mu\\
    =&a(n+h)\overline{a(n)}\int \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n+h) ]}h_{i,N}\cdot T^{[ f_i(n) ]}\overline{h_{i,N}} ~d\mu\\
    =&a(n+h)\overline{a(n)}\int \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n+h) ]-[ f_1(n) ]}h_{i,N}\cdot T^{[ f_i(n) ]-[ f_1(n) ]}\overline{h_{i,N}}~d\mu
\end{align*}
by the fact that $T$ is measure preserving. Recall that for any $x,y\in \mathbb{R}$, $[ x ]-[ y ]-[ x-y ] \in \{-1,0,1\}$. For $n\in \mathbb{N}$, let the vector $v(n)\in \{-1,0,1\}^{2\ell}$ be equal to
$$
(([ f_i(n) ]-[ f_1(n) ]-[ f_i(n)-f_1(n) ])_{i=1}^{\ell},([ f_i(n+h) ]-[ f_1(n) ]-[ f_i(n+h)-f_1(n) ])_{i=1}^{\ell}).
$$
 Writing $S(\beta) = \{n\in \mathbb{N}: v(n) = \beta\}$ for $\beta\in \{-1,0,1\}^{2\ell} $, we have that $(S(\beta))_{\beta\in \{-1,0,1\}^{2\ell}}$ is a partition of $\mathbb{N}$ and so to prove (\ref{eq:vdc_goal}), it suffices to show 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:vdc_goal_2}
    \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(1_{S(\beta)}(n)\cdot \int u_N(n+m)\cdot \overline{u_N(n)}~d\mu\right)=0
\end{equation}
for each $\beta \in  \{-1,0,1\}^{2\ell} $. Fix $\beta= (\beta_1,\dots, \beta_{2\ell}) \in \{-1,0,1\}^{2\ell}$.



Now put $F_i(x)  = [ f_i(x)-f_1(x) ]$ and $F_{\ell+i}(x) = [ f_i(x+h)-f_1(x) ]$ for $i=1,\dots, \ell$, and put $g_{i,N} = T^{\beta_i}h_{i,N}$ and $g_{k+i,N} = T^{\beta_{k+i}}\overline{h_{i,N}}$ for $i=1,\dots, \ell$, so that
\begin{align*}
    \int u_N(n+h)\cdot \overline{u_N(n)} ~d\mu= &a(n+h)\overline{a(n)}\int \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n+h)-f_1(n) ]}g_{i,N}\cdot T^{[ f_i(n)-f_1(n) ]}{g_{k+i,N}}~d\mu\\
    =&a(n+h)\overline{a(n)}\int \prod_{i=1}^{2\ell}T^{[ F_{i}(n) ]}g_{i,N}~d\mu
\end{align*}
for $n\in S(\beta)$. Next, rewrite (\ref{eq:vdc_goal_2}) as 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:vdc_goal_3}
        \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\tilde{a}(n) \int \prod_{i=1}^{2\ell}T^{[ F_{i}(n) ]}g_{i,N}~d\mu\right)=0
\end{equation}
where $\tilde{a}(n) = 1_{S(\beta)}(n)\cdot a(n+h)\overline{a(n)}$.


Let $G_1,\dots, G_{\ell'}$ be a relabeling of the unbounded elements of $\{F_1,\dots, F_{2k}\}$ such that $|G_1|\preceq \cdots \preceq |G_{\ell'}|$. As above, if $\lim_{x\to\infty}|F_i(x)|<\infty$ then we can factor terms of the form $T^{\lim_{x\to\infty}[F_i(x)]}g_{i,N}+o_{N\to\infty}(1)$ out of the left-hand side of (\ref{eq:4.2_goal}). So it suffices to show that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:vdc_goal_4}
        \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\tilde{a}(n) \int \prod_{i=1}^{\ell'}T^{[ G_{i}(n) ]}g_{i,N}~d\mu\right)=0
\end{equation}
holds.


Let $\mathscr{F}' = \{G_1,\dots, G_{\ell'}\}$. Next, we check that $\mathscr{F}'$ satisfies the induction hypothesis, which will complete the proof by showing that (\ref{eq:vdc_goal_4}) holds and that we can take the same value of $s$ for $\mathscr{F}$ as we can for $\mathscr{F}'$.

To show that $\lim_{x\to\infty}|G_{\ell'}(x)|=\lim_{x\to\infty}|G_{\ell'}(x)-G_j(x)| = \infty$ for all $j<\ell'$, recall that 
$$
\lim_{x\to\infty}|F_{2k}(x)-F_j(x)|  = \begin{cases} 
\lim_{x\to\infty}|f_{2k}(x+h)-f_j(x)| &\text{ if }  j\in \{1,\dots, k-1\}\\
\lim_{x\to\infty}|f_k(x+h)-f_j(x+h)| &\text{ if }  j\in \{k+1,\dots, 2k-1\}
\end{cases}
$$
which tends to $\infty$ by assumption in all cases. If $j=k$ then $\lim_{x\to\infty}|F_{2k}(x)-F_k(x)|  = \lim_{x\to\infty}|f_k(x+h)-f_k(x)|=\infty$ because $\deg(f_k) = \max\{d\in \mathbb{N}: m_d(\mathscr{F})\}\geqslant 2$.


Next, it is easy to check that $\mathscr{F}'$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) because of the fact that $\mathscr{F}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}).


Lastly, we need to show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:char_vectors}
(m_1(\mathscr{F}'),\dots, m_{d_{\max}(\mathscr{F}')}({\mathscr{F}}'))<(m_1(\mathscr{F}),\dots, m_{d_{\max}(\mathscr{F})}({\mathscr{F}})).
\end{equation}


If $f_1\not\sim_{\text{PET}} f_i$, then $F_{k+i}\sim_{\text{PET}} F_i \sim_{\text{PET}} f_i$. If $f_1\not\sim_{\text{PET}} f_i$ and $f_1\not\sim_{\text{PET}} f_j$ then $f_i\sim_{\text{PET}} f_j$ holds if and only if $F_{i}\sim_{\text{PET}} F_j$. So it follows that $m_d(\mathscr{F}) = m_{d}(\mathscr{F}')$ for $d>\deg(f_1)\geqslant 1$. But $m_{\deg(f_1)}(\mathscr{F})>m_{\deg(f_1)}(\mathscr{F}')$ because $\deg(f_i-f_1)<\deg(f_i)$ whenever $f_i\sim_{\text{PET}} f_1$. This shows that (\ref{eq:char_vectors}) holds and so by the induction hypothesis,
we are done.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- theorem-20: body_md -->
\label{thm:true_for_nilsystem}
 Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field. Let $f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\in \mathcal{H}$ be  subpolynomial and let $W\in \mathcal{H}$ satisfy $1\prec \log W(x)\prec x$. Suppose that $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}). Let $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$ be a nilsystem where $X= G/\Gamma$ for $G$ simply connected. 
     \begin{enumerate}[label = (\roman*)]
         \item For each $g_1,\dots, g_{\ell}\in C(X)$ and each $x\in X$,
         \begin{equation}\label{eq:new_eq_1}
             \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_1(n) ]}g_1(x)\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}g_{\ell}(x))
         \end{equation}
         exists.
        \item Suppose that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}\cap \mathbb{Z}[x]=\{0\}$. Then for each $g_1,\dots, g_{\ell}\in C(X)$ and each $x\in X$,
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:new_eq_2}
             \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_1(n) ]}g_1(x)\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}g_{\ell}(x)) = \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}g_i(T^nx)\right).
         \end{equation}
         \item 
         Suppose that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ is jointly intersective.
         Then for any $A\in \mathscr{B}_X$ with $\mu_X(A)>0$,
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:new_eq_3}
             \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\mu_X(A\cap T^{-[ f_1(n) ]}A\cdots T^{-[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}A) >0.
         \end{equation}
     \end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-20 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:bmr_sec_5}
   Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field, let $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}$ be an arbitrary collection of functions, and let $W\in \mathcal{H}$ such that $1\prec \log W(x)\prec x$. 
   Consider the following statements:
   \begin{enumerate}
    \item[$(i)$] For any nilsystem $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$with $X =G/\Gamma$ for $G$ simply connected, for any $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in C(X)$ and any $x\in X$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_1}
    \text{ the limit }\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n) ]}h_i(x)\right)\text{ exists.}
\end{equation}
       \item[$(i)'$] (\ref{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_1}) holds in $L^2(X)$ for any ergodic nilsystem $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$ and any $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$.
       \item[$(i)''$] (\ref{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_1}) holds in $L^2(X)$ for any invertible measure preserving system $(X,\mathscr{B},\mu,T)$ and any $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$.
       \item[$(ii)$] For each nilsystem $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$ with $X =G/\Gamma$ for $G$ simply connected, for each $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in C(X)$, and for each $x\in X$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_2}
   \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n) ]}h_i(x)\right) = \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}h_i^*(x)
\end{equation}
 where $h_i^*$ is the projection in $L^2(X)$ of $h_i$ onto the subspace of $T$ invariant functions (in this case we have $h_i^*(x) = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}h_i(T^nx)$ since nilsystems are uniquely ergodic).
       \item[$(ii)'$] (\ref{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_2}) holds in $L^2(X)$ for any ergodic nilsystem $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$ and any $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$.
       \item[$(ii)''$] (\ref{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_2}) holds in $L^2(X)$ for any invertible measure preserving system $(X,\mathscr{B},\mu,T)$ and any $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$.
       \item[$(iii)$] For any nilsystem $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$ with $X =G/\Gamma$ for $G$ simply connected and any nonzero $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in C(X)$ taking values in $[0,\infty)$,        \begin{equation}\label{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_3}
    \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}T^{[ f_i(n) ]}h_i(x)\right)>0.
       \end{equation}
       \item[$(iii)'$] (\ref{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_3}) holds for any ergodic nilsystem $(X,\mathscr{B}_X,\mu_X,T)$ and any nonzero $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$ taking values in $[0,\infty)$.
       \item[$(iii)''$] (\ref{eq:BMR_sec_5_lemma_3}) holds for any invertible measure preserving system $(X,\mathscr{B},\mu,T)$ and any nonzero $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$ taking values in $[0,\infty)$.
   \end{enumerate}
Then the implications $(i)\implies (i)'$, $(ii)\implies (ii)'$, $(iii)\implies (iii)'$ hold. With the additional assumption that there exists an $s\in \mathbb{N}$ such that such that (\ref{eq:4.2_goal}) holds for any invertible measure preserving system $(X,\mathcal{B},\mu,T)$ and any $h_{\ell}\in L^\infty(X)$ with $\|h_{\ell}\|_s=0$, the implications $(i)'\implies (i)''$, $(ii)'\implies (ii)''$, $(iii)'\implies (iii)''$ also hold.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:variant_of_modulo_averages}
    Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a maximal 
    Hardy field and let $W\in \mathcal{H}$ with $1\prec \log W(x)\prec x$. Let $(a(n))_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ be a bounded sequence and let $R\in \mathbb{N}$. Then
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)$ exists if and only if $\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{R}\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(nR+r)$ exists. Moreover, these two limits are equal when they both exist.
        \item Suppose that $a(n)\geqslant 0$ for all $n\in \mathbb{N}$, $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)$ exists, and for some $r_0\in \mathbb{Z}$ with $0\leqslant r_0\leqslant R-1$, the limit $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(nR+r_0)$ exists and is positive. Then $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)>0$.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
First note that 
\begin{align*}
&\frac{1}{R}\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(nR+r) = \frac{1}{R}\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\frac{1}{W(N)}\sum_{n=1}^N\Delta W(n)a(nR+r)\\ = &\frac{1}{W(NR/R)}\sum_{n=1}^{NR}\frac{\Delta W(\lfloor n/R\rfloor)}{R}a(n)+o_{N\to\infty}(1) = \operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant NR}^Va(n)+o_{N\to\infty}(1)
\end{align*}
for $V(N) = W(N/R)$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:silverman_toeplitz_application_1}
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n) = \sum_{n=1}^Nc_{N,n}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{k\leqslant n}^Va(k)+o_{N\to\infty}(1)
\end{equation}
where $c_{N,N} =\frac{V(N)\Delta W(N)}{W(N)\Delta V(N)}$, $c_{N,n} = \left(\frac{\Delta W(n)}{\Delta V(n)}-\frac{\Delta W(n+1)}{\Delta V(n+1)}\right)\frac{\Delta V(n)}{W(N)}$ for $n<N$, and $c_{N,n} = 0$ for $n>N$. Observe that 
\begin{align}
    \lim_{N\to\infty}c_{N,N} =& \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{V(N)\Delta W(N)}{W(N)\Delta V(N)} = \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{V(N)\cdot  W'(N)}{W(N) \cdot V'(N)}\\
    =&\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{(\log W)'(N)}{(\log V)'(N)} =  \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\log (W(N))}{\log (V(N))} \label{eq:limit_in_avaerging_lemma}
\end{align}
by L'H\^opital's rule. We know that the limit in (\ref{eq:limit_in_avaerging_lemma}) exists since $\log W$ and $\log V$ belong to the same Hardy field, and the limit must be larger than $1$ because $W$ is eventually increasing and so $\log W$ is eventually larger than $\log V$. Additionally, this limit is finite since $\log (W(x))\prec x$ and so $\log (W(x/R))\geqslant \log (W(x))/R$. 


It is clear from (\ref{eq:silverman_toeplitz_application_1}) that $\sum_{n=1}^Nc_{N,n} = 1+o_{N\to\infty}(1)$ by taking the sequence $a(k) = 1$ for all $k$. Additionally, for $n<N$, $c_{N,n}$ is built out of functions which belong to the same Hardy field and so it must be eventually positive or eventually negative. Given that $c_{N,N}$ is larger than $1$, we conclude that $c_{N,n}< 0$ for all $N$ and all sufficiently large $n<N$. Hence 
\begin{equation}
\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sum_{n=1}^N|c_{N,n}| \leqslant \limsup_{N\to\infty}\left(c_{N,N}-\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}c_{N,n}\right) \leqslant \lim_{N\to\infty}c_{N,N}+\left(\lim_{N\to\infty}c_{N,N}-1\right)<\infty.
\end{equation}
We have shown the conditions of Lemma \ref{lem:silverman_toeplitz} hold and so $(c_{N,n})_{N,n\in \mathbb{N}}$ defines a regular method of summation. This shows that if the limit $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Va(n)$ exists then $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)$ exists and they are equal. The other direction follows from a similar argument, since
\[
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Va(n) = \sum_{n=1}^Nd_{N,n}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{k\leqslant n}^Wa(k)+o_{N\to\infty}(1)
\]
where $d_{N,N} =\frac{W(N)\Delta V(N)}{V(N)\Delta W(N)}$, $d_{N,n} = \left(\frac{\Delta V(n)}{\Delta W(n)}-\frac{\Delta V(n+1)}{\Delta W(n+1)}\right)\frac{\Delta W(n)}{V(N)}$, and $d_{N,n}=0$ for $n>N$. By similar reasoning as above, $\lim_{N\to\infty}d_{N,N}$ exists and is less than $1$, and $\sum_{n=1}^Nd_{N,n}= 1+o_{N\to\infty}(1)$. It follows that $d_{N,n}$ is positive for all $n\leqslant N$ and all large enough $N$ and so $\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sum_{n=1}^N|d_{n,N}|=1<\infty$. Again by Lemma \ref{lem:silverman_toeplitz}, $(d_{N,n})_{N,n\in \mathbb{N}}$ defines a regular method of summation and this proves the first item in the statement of the lemma.

The second item follows from the first. Indeed,
\[
\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(n)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{R}\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(nR+r)\geqslant \frac{1}{R}\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wa(nR+r_0)>0.
\]
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- theorem-21: body_md -->
\label{thm:true_for_nilsystems_and_also}
    Theorem \ref{thm:true_for_nilsystem} is true when each function $f_i$ satisfies the additional assumption that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:additional_assumption}
    \text{if }|f_i(t)-p(t)|\to 0\text{ for some }p(t)\in \mathbb{R}[t] \text{ with }p(t)-p(0)\in \mathbb{Q}[t], \text{ then }p(t)-p(0)\in \mathbb{Z}[t].
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-21 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Let $I = \{i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}: f_i$ does not satisfy (\ref{eq:additional_assumption})$\}$.
    For $i\in I$, pick $p_i(t)\in \mathbb{R}[t]$ such that $p_i(t)-p_i(0)\in \mathbb{Q}[t]$ and $f_i(t) = p_i(t)+o_{t\to\infty}(1)$. Pick $R\in \mathbb{N}$ such that $R\cdot (p_i(t)-p_i(0))\in \mathbb{Z}[t]$. 

    Let $r\in \mathbb{N}$ be arbitrary. For $i=1,\dots, \ell$, define $f_{i,r}(x) = f_i(Rx+r)$ and note that $\{f_{1,r},\dots, f_{\ell,r}\}$ satisfies (\ref{eq:additional_assumption}) by our choice of $R$. Indeed, if $i\not\in I$ then $f_{i,r}$ satisfies (\ref{eq:additional_assumption}) because $f_i$ satisfies (\ref{eq:additional_assumption}), and if $i\in I$ then $f_{i,r}(t) = p_{i}(Rt+r)$ and $p_{i}(Rt+r)-p_i(r)\in \mathbb{Z}[t]$. Additionally, $\{f_{1,r},\dots, f_{\ell,r}\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) since $\{f_{1},\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}).
    


Let $(X,\mathscr{B},\mu,T)$ be a nilsystem with $X=G/\Gamma$ for $G$ simply connected. By Theorem \ref{thm:true_for_nilsystems_and_also}, the limit 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:reduction_eq_1}
\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_{1,r}(n) ]}h_1\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell,r}(n) ]}h_{\ell})
    \end{equation}
    exists in $L^2(X)$ for all $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$ and for all $r\in \mathbb{N}$. Hence the limit 
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:reduction_eq_2}
\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{R}\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_{1,r}(n) ]}h_1\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell,r}(n) ]}h_{\ell})
    \end{equation}
    exists and equals (\ref{eq:reduction_eq_1}) for all $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$. By the first item in Lemma \ref{lem:variant_of_modulo_averages}, the limit 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reduction_eq_3}
\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_{1}(n) ]}h_1\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}h_{\ell})
\end{equation}
exists and equals (\ref{eq:reduction_eq_2}) for all $h_1,\dots, h_{\ell}\in L^{\infty}(X)$. This shows that items (i) and (ii) of Theorem \ref{thm:true_for_nilsystem} hold in this case.


Lastly, suppose that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ is jointly intersective. Let $q_1(t),\dots, q_{m}(t)\in \mathbb{Z}[t]$ be such that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}\subseteq \text{Span}\{q_1,\dots, q_{m}\}$ and for each $r\in \mathbb{N}$ there exists $n\in \mathbb{N}$ with $q_i(n)$ divisible by $r$ for all $i$. Now pick $r_0\in \{0,\dots, R-1\}$ such that the functions $Q_{i}(t) = q_i(Rt+r_0)$ are jointly intersective for $i=1,\dots, m$. Such an $r_0$ exists because for any $M\in \mathbb{N}$ there is an $n\in \mathbb{N}$ such that $q_i(n)$ is divisible by $MR$ for all $i$, from which it follows that $q_i(n)$ is divisible by both $M$ and $R$ for all $i$. We also know that $q_i(n_1)\equiv q_i(n_2)\operatorname{mod} R$ if $n_1\equiv n_2\operatorname{mod} R$ and so it follows that there exists $r_0$ such that $q_i(Rk+r_0)\equiv 0\operatorname{mod} R$ for all $i$ and all $k\in \mathbb{N}$, so that for each $M\in \mathbb{N}$ there is a $k\in \mathbb{N}$ with $q_i(Rk+r_0)\equiv 0\operatorname{mod} M$. 


This shows that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_{1,r_0},\dots, f_{\ell,r_0}\}$ is jointly intersective and so by Theorem \ref{thm:main} we have
\begin{equation*}             \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\mu(A\cap T^{-[ f_{1,r_0}(n) ]}A\cdots T^{-[ f_{\ell,r_0}(n) ]}A) >0.
\end{equation*}
for any $A\in \mathscr{B}$ with $\mu(A)>0$. By the second item in Lemma \ref{lem:variant_of_modulo_averages}, it follows that
\begin{equation*}             \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\mu(A\cap T^{-[ f_{1}(n) ]}A\cdots T^{-[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}A) >0.
\end{equation*}
and so we are done.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- theorem-22: body_md -->
\label{thm:BMR_uniform_distribution_result}
Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group, let
$\Gamma \subseteq G$ be a uniform and discrete subgroup, let
$X = G/\Gamma$, and let $a \in G$. Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and let $f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell} \in \mathcal{H}$ be subpolynomial functions which satisfy (\ref{eq:additional_assumption}). Let $W \in \mathcal{H}$ with $1 \prec \log W(x) \prec x$. Suppose that property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) holds. Then there exists a Borel probability measure $\nu_a$ on
$X^{\ell}$ such that the sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BMR_5.2}
n \mapsto \bigl(a^{ [ f_1(n) ]},
a^{ [ f_2(n) ]},\ldots,
a^{ [ f_{\ell}(n) ]}\bigr)\Gamma^{\ell}
\end{equation}
is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $(X^{\ell},\nu)$.


Moreover, if $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ is jointly intersective then the point
$1_{X^{\ell}} = 1_G\Gamma^{\ell}$ belongs to the support of $\nu_a$, and if $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}\cap \mathbb{Z}[x]=\{0\}$ then $\nu_a$ is the Haar measure on the
subnilmanifold $Y_a^{\ell} \subseteq X^{\ell}$, where $Y_a = \overline{\{a^n\Gamma : n \in \mathbb{Z}\}}$.
<!-- end theorem-22 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group, let
$\Gamma \subseteq G$ be a uniform and discrete subgroup, let
$X = G/\Gamma$, and let $T:X\rightarrow X$ be given by $T(x\Gamma) =  ax\Gamma$ for some $a \in G$.



Let $x\in X$ and let $\gamma_x\in G$ denote a coset representative for $x$, so that $x=\gamma_x\Gamma$. Let $a_x \gamma_x^{-1}a\gamma_x$ so that $a^{[ f_i(n) ]}x = \gamma_x(\gamma_x^{-1}a\gamma_x)^{[ f_i(n) ]}\Gamma = \gamma_x a_x^{[ f_i(n) ]}\Gamma$ for all $n\in \mathbb{N}$ and all $i$. Let $g_1,\dots, g_{\ell}\in C(X)$ and define $G_{x}(x_1,\dots, x_k) = g_1(\gamma_xx_1)\cdots g_{\ell}(\gamma_xx_{\ell})$. Then by (\ref{eq:BMR_5.2}) we have that
 \begin{align*}
             &\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_1(n) ]}g_1(x)\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}g_{\ell}(x)) = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wg_1(a^{[ f_1(n) ]}x)\cdots g_{\ell}(a^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}x)\\
             =&\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^Wg_1(\gamma_x a_x^{[ f_1(n) ]}\Gamma)\cdots g_{\ell}(\gamma_x a_x^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}\Gamma)\\
             =& \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^WG_x(a_x^{[ f_1(n) ]},\dots, a_x^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}) = \int_{X^{\ell}} G_x ~d\nu_{a_x}.
\end{align*}
This shows that $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(T^{[ f_1(n) ]}g_1(x)\cdots T^{[ f_{\ell}(n) ]}g_{\ell}(x))$ exists. Next, suppose that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}\cap \mathbb{Z}[x]=\{0\}$. Then $\nu_{a_x}$ is the Haar measure on $Y_x = \overline{\{a_x^n\Gamma:n\in \mathbb{Z}\}}$ and so  
\begin{align*}
    \int_{X^{\ell}} G_x ~d\nu_{a_x} = \int_{Y_{a_x}^{\ell}} G_x ~d\nu_{a_x}=&\int_{Y_{a_x}^{\ell}} \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g_i(\gamma_x x_i)~d\nu_x(x_1,\dots, x_{\ell}) \\=&\prod_{i=1}^{\ell} \int_{Y_{a_x}} g_i(\gamma_x x_i)~d\mu_{Y_{a_x}}(x_i)
\end{align*}
    but we know that 
    \[
    \int_{Y_{a_x}} g_i(\gamma_x x_i)~d\mu_{Y_{a_x}}(x_i)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}g_i(\gamma_xa_x^n\Gamma) =  \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}g_i(a^nx)
    \]
    for each $i$, and so we have that
    \begin{align*}
    \int_{X^{\ell}} G_x ~d\nu_{a_x} = \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}g_i(a^nx)
\end{align*}
as desired. 


Lastly, suppose that $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ is jointly intersective. Let $g\in C(x)$ satisfy $g(x)\geqslant 0$ for all $x\in X$ and $\int_X g~d\mu_X>0$. We will show that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:u.d._reduction_goal}
    \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\int_X g(x)\cdot \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(a^{[ f_i(n) ]}x)~d\mu_X(x) >0
\end{equation}
so that (\ref{eq:new_eq_3}) follows by approximating the step function $1_A$ by continuous functions. By the dominated convergence theorem, we have that
\[
\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\int_X g(x) \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(a^{[ f_i(n) ]}x)~d\mu_X(x)=\int_X g(x) \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(a^{[ f_i(n) ]}x)\right)d\mu_X(x) 
\]
and we recall from the above arguments that $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(a^{[ f_i(n) ]}x)$ converges to $\int_{X^{\ell}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(\gamma_x x_i)~d\nu_{x}(x_1,\dots, x_{\ell})$. From Theorem \ref{thm:BMR_uniform_distribution_result}, $1_{G^{\ell}}\Gamma$ is in the support of $\nu_{a_x}$ and so $\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(\gamma_x 1_{G}\Gamma) = \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(x) =g(x)^{\ell}>0$ whenever $g(x)>0$ and so the fact that $\int_{X^{\ell}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g(\gamma_x x_i)~d\nu_{x}(x_1,\dots, x_{\ell})$ is positive follows from the fact that $\int g ~d\mu_X$ is positive. This concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:floor_removal}
Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group, let
$\Gamma \subseteq G$ be a uniform and discrete subgroup, let
$X = G/\Gamma$, and let $a \in G$. Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and let $f_1,\ldots,f_{\ell} \in \mathcal{H}$ be subpolynomial functions which satisfy (\ref{eq:additional_assumption}). Let $W \in \mathcal{H}$ with $1 \prec \log W(x) \prec x$. Suppose that $\nu$ is a Borel probability measure on $X^{\ell}$ such that the sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BMR_no_floor}
n \mapsto \bigl(a^{ {f_1(n)}},
a^{ {f_2(n)}},\ldots,
a^{ {f_{\ell}(n)}}\bigr)\Gamma^{\ell}.
\end{equation}
is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $(X^{\ell},\nu)$. Then the sequence
\begin{equation}
n \mapsto \bigl(a^{ [ f_1(n) ]},
a^{ [ f_2(n) ]},\ldots,
a^{ [ f_{\ell}(n) ]}\bigr)\Gamma^{\ell}
\end{equation}
is also uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $(X^{\ell},\nu)$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
This proof relies on Theorem \ref{thm_generalized_richter_D}, which will be proven in Section \ref{section:richter_results}.

    Without loss of generality, assume that $\overline{\{a^n\Gamma:n\in \mathbb{Z}\}} = X$. Let $a_1 = (a\Gamma,\Gamma,\dots,\Gamma)\in X^{\ell}$, $a_2 = (\Gamma,a\Gamma,\Gamma,\dots,\Gamma)\in X^{\ell}, \dots, a_{\ell} = (\Gamma,\dots,\Gamma,a\Gamma)\in X^{\ell}$, so that 
    \[
    v(n) = a_1^{f_1(n)}\cdots a_{\ell}^{f_{\ell}(n)} \Gamma^{\ell}= (a^{f_1(n)},\dots, a^{f_{\ell}}(n))\Gamma^{\ell}
    \]
    is a sequence in $X^{\ell}$. By Theorem \ref{thm_generalized_richter_D}, there exists $q\in \mathbb{N}$ and closed and connected submanifolds $Y_0,\dots,Y_{q-1}$ such that $(v(qn+r))_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $Y_r$. Let $\nu_a = \frac{1}{q}\sum_{r=0}^{q-1}\mu_{Y_r}$. Then $(v(n))_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $(X,
    \nu_a)$ by Lemma \ref{lem:variant_of_modulo_averages}. From Lemma \ref{lem:floor_removal}, it follows that the sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BMR_5.2_again}
n \mapsto \bigl(a^{ [ f_1(n) ]},
a^{ [ f_2(n) ]},\ldots,
a^{ [ f_{\ell}(n) ]}\bigr)\Gamma^{\ell}
\end{equation}
is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $(X^{\ell},\nu_a)$.


The remainder of Theorem \ref{thm:BMR_uniform_distribution_result} follows from \cite[Theorem 5.7]{BMR24}. More specifically, the statement of \cite[Theorem 5.7]{BMR24} is the same as the statement of Theorem \ref{thm:BMR_uniform_distribution_result} except it is assumed that $1\prec W(x)\preceq x$ and that the functions $f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}$ satisfy property (P) (see Definition \ref{def:property_P}) instead of property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}). By \cite[Corollary A.5]{Richter23} there exists a function $\widetilde{W}\in \mathcal{H}$ such that $1\prec \widetilde{W}(x)\preceq x$ and the functions $f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}$ satisfy property (P) for $\widetilde{W}$ (note that property (P) is called property $(P_W)$ in \cite{Richter23}). We assume that $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{\log \widetilde{W}(x)}{\log W(x)} = 0$, since otherwise we can replace $\mathcal{H}$ with a maximal Hardy field containing $\mathcal{H}$ and we can replace $\widetilde{W}$ with $\log {W}$. 


Then by \cite[Theorem 5.7]{BMR24} there exists a probability measure $\nu^*$ such that the sequence (\ref{eq:BMR_5.2_again}) is uniformly distributed in $(X^{\ell},\nu^*)$ with respect to $\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{\widetilde{W}}$. Additionally, if $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ is jointly intersective then the point
$1_{X^{\ell}} = 1_G\Gamma^{\ell}$ belongs to the support of $\nu^*$, and if $\operatorname{Poly}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}\cap \mathbb{Z}[x]=\{0\}$ then $\nu^*$ is the Haar measure on the
subnilmanifold $Y_a^{\ell} \subseteq X^{\ell}$, where $Y_a = \overline{\{a^n\Gamma : n \in \mathbb{Z}\}}$.

Since (\ref{eq:BMR_5.2_again}) is uniformly distributed in $(X^{\ell},\nu_a)$ with respect to $W$-averages, it is uniformly distributed in $(X^{\ell},\nu_a)$ with respect to $\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{\widetilde{W}}$ by Theorem \ref{thm:mikey_thm} and our assumption that $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{\log \widetilde{W}(x)}{\log W(x)} = 0$. But  (\ref{eq:BMR_5.2_again}) is also uniformly distributed in $(X^{\ell},\nu^*)$ with respect to $\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{\widetilde{W}}$ and hence it must be that $\nu^* = \nu_a$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- theorem-23: body_md -->
\label{thm_generalized_richter_D}
Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and let $W\in \mathcal{H}$ satisfy $1\prec \log W(x)\prec x$. Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group, let $\Gamma$ a uniform and discrete subgroup of $G$, and assume $X = G/\Gamma$ is connected.
Suppose
$$
v(n) = a_1^{f_1(n)}\cdots a_{\ell}^{f_{\ell}(n)}\quad \text{ for all }n\in \mathbb{N}
$$
where $a_1,\dots , a_{\ell} \in G$ are commuting, and $f_1,\dots , f_{\ell} \in \mathcal{H}$ are subpolynomial functions which satisfy property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}). Then there exists a closed and connected subgroup $H$ of $G$, $q\in \mathbb{N}$, and points $x_0, x_1,\dots , x_{q-1} \in X$ such that $Y_r = H{x_r}$ is a closed sub-nilmanifold of $X$ and $(v(qn + r)\Gamma)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly
distributed with respect to $W$-averages in $Y_r$ for all $r = 0, 1, \dots , q - 1$.
<!-- end theorem-23 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:replace_richter_6.4}
     Let $W,f$ be functions which belong to the same Hardy field and suppose that $1\prec \log W(x)\prec f(x)\prec x$. Pick $\xi \in (0,1]$ and define $K_n = \mathbb{N}\cap f^{-1}(\xi n, \xi (n+1))$. Also, put $g(n) = f(n)/\xi$, $p_n = \sum_{i\in K_n}\Delta W(i)$, and $P_N = \sum_{n=1}^Np_n$. Then
     \begin{enumerate}[label = (\roman*)]
         \item $\frac{\Delta (W\circ g^{-1})(n)}{p_n}= 1 +o_{n\to\infty}(1)$,  
         \item $\frac{(W\circ g^{-1})(N)}{P_N}= 1 +o_{N\to\infty}(1)$, 
         \item $\lim_{N\to\infty}P_N=\infty$, 
         \item $\frac{p_N}{P_N}=o_{N\to\infty}(1)$.
     \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Define $G(k) = \max\{n\in \mathbb{N}: {g(n)}\leqslant k\}
= \max\{n\in \mathbb{N}: f(n)\leqslant \xi k\}$ for $k\in \mathbb{N}$. For large enough $n$, $K_n = \{G(n)+1,\dots, G(n+1)\}$ and so $p_n = W(G(n+1))-W(G(n)) = \Delta (W\circ G)(n+1)$. Additionally, note that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6.4_1}
    g^{-1}(k-1)\leqslant G(k) \leqslant g^{-1}(k)
\end{equation} 
for all sufficiently large $k$. By assumption, we have $1\prec \log(W\circ g^{-1})(x)\prec x$ from which it follows that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:6.4_2}
    \lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\Delta \log(W\circ g^{-1})(k-1)}{\Delta \log(W\circ g^{-1})(k)}=1.
\end{equation}
Combining (\ref{eq:6.4_1}) and (\ref{eq:6.4_2}) with the fact that $W$ and $g^{-1}$ are eventually monotone, we have $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Delta (W\circ g^{-1})(n-1)}{p_{n-1}}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Delta (W\circ g^{-1})(n-1)}{\Delta (W\circ G)(n)}=1$. This shows that statement (i) holds.

By Theorem \ref{thm:stolz_cesaro} we also have $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{ (W\circ g^{-1})(n)}{ (W\circ \hat{s})(n)}=1$, which is statement (ii). Statement (iii) follows from (ii) since we know that $(W\circ g^{-1})$ tends to $\infty$, and (iv) follows from (i) and (ii) since we know that 
\begin{equation}
    \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{\Delta (W\circ g^{-1})(x)}{(W\circ g^{-1})(x)} =  \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{\Delta \log (W\circ g^{-1})(x)}{\Delta x}
 = \lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{ \log (W\circ g^{-1})(x)}{ x} = 0.
 \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- theorem-24: body_md -->
\label{thm:funny_vdc}
Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field and suppose that $W,g_1,\dots, g_{m}\in \mathcal{H}$ with 
\begin{equation}
    1\prec \log W(x)\prec g_1(x)\prec g_2(x)\prec\cdots \prec g_{m}(x)\prec x.
\end{equation}
Let $\Psi:\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ be bounded and uniformly continuous. Suppose that for each $\varepsilon\in \mathbb{R}$, the limit 
\begin{equation*}
    A({\varepsilon}) = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\Psi(g_1(n),\dots, g_{m-1}(n),g_{m}(n)+\varepsilon)\cdot \overline{\Psi(g_1(n),\dots, g_{m-1}(n),g_{m}(n))}\right)
\end{equation*}
exists. Suppose also that for each $\delta>0$, there is an $\xi\in (0,\delta)$ with $\lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}A(\xi h) = 0$. Then 
\begin{equation}
    \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\Psi(g_1(n),\dots, g_{m}(n))=0.
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-24 -->
<!-- theorem-25: body_md -->
\label{thm:generalized_G}
    Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field. Let $g_1,\dots, g_{m}\in \mathcal{H}$ be subpolynomial and let $W\in \mathcal{H}$ with $1\prec \log W(x)\prec x$.
    Let $G$ be a simply connected nilpotent Lie group, $\Gamma$ a uniform and discrete subgroup of $G$. Define $v:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow G$ by 
    \begin{equation}
        v(n) = z_1^{g_1(n)}\cdots z_{m}^{g_{m}(n)}s_1^{p_1(n)}\cdots s_M^{p_M(n)},
    \end{equation}
    where $z_1,\dots, z_{m}, s_1\dots, s_M\in G$, are pairwise commuting, for each $i$ the set $\overline{s_i^{\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma}$ is a connected subnilmanifold of $X = G/\Gamma$, and $p_1,\dots, p_m\in \mathbb{Q}[x]$. Additionally, assume the following:
    \begin{enumerate}[label =(\arabic*)]
        \item $p_j(\mathbb{Z})\subseteq \mathbb{Z}$ for all $j=1,\dots, M$,
        \item $\deg(p_j)=j$ for all $j=1,\dots, M$,
        \item $g_1(x)\prec g_2(x)\prec \cdots \prec g_{m}(x)$,
        \item \label{condition_(4)} for each horizontal character $\eta:X\rightarrow \mathbb{T}$ which is nontrivial on $\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_{m}^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}$ we have $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^W_{n\leqslant N}\eta(v(n)\Gamma)=0$,
        \item if $g\in \{g_1,\dots, g_{\ell}\}$ has $\deg(g)\geqslant 2$ then $g'\in \{g_1,\dots, g_{\ell}\}$.
    \end{enumerate}


    Then $(v(n)\Gamma)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed with respect to $W$-averages in the sub-nilmanifold $\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_{m}^{\mathbb{R}}\cdot s_1^{\mathbb{Z}}\cdots s_M^{\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma}$.
<!-- end theorem-25 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
Condition \ref{condition_(4)} in Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} is the only part which is not an immediate generalization of \cite[Theorem G]{Richter23}. It replaces the assumption made in \cite[Theorem G]{Richter23} that $W$ is compatible with each $f_i$, which cannot hold in our context since compatibility with $W$ is not preserved under taking derivatives whenever $\log(x)\preceq \log W(x)$. However, condition \ref{condition_(4)} could replace the $W$ compatibility condition in \cite[Theorem G]{Richter23} and the proofs of \cite{Richter23} would remain unchanged.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:abelian_case}
    Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} is true when $G$ is abelian.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
It is known that any connected abelian Lie group is isomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^{d}$ for some $d\geqslant 0$. Then $\Gamma$ must be isomorphic to $\mathbb{Z}^{d}$, so without loss of generality assume $G = \mathbb{R}^d$ and $\Gamma = \mathbb{Z}^{d}$.


The horizontal characters $\eta:\mathbb{R}^d/\mathbb{Z}^d\rightarrow \mathbb{T}$ are precisely the nonzero central characters $\varphi:\mathbb{R}^d/\mathbb{Z}^d\rightarrow \mathbb{C}\backslash \{0\}$. So, in order to show that
\begin{equation*}
    v(n) \operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^d= z_1g_1(n)+\cdots +z_{m}g_{m}(n)+s_1p_1(n)+\dots+s_Mp_M(n)\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^d
\end{equation*}
is uniformly distributed in $T:=\overline{\mathbb{R} z_1+\cdots \mathbb{R} z_{m}+\mathbb{Z} s_1+\cdots \mathbb{Z} s_{M}\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^{d}}$ with respect to $W$-averages it suffices to show that $ \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\eta( v(n)\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^d) = 0$ for each nontrival horizontal character $\eta:T\rightarrow \mathbb{T}$ by the Weyl criterion (see Lemma \ref{lem:richter_equivalence}). However, this is guaranteed by condition \ref{condition_(4)} of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G}. Indeed, when $\eta$ is is nontrivial on $\overline{\mathbb{R} z_1+\cdots \mathbb{R} z_{m}\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^{d}}$ we have $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\eta( v(n)\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^d)=0$. So we may suppose that $\eta$ is identically equal to $1$ on $\overline{\mathbb{R} z_1+\cdots \mathbb{R} z_{m}\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^{d}}$. Then 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weyl_criterion_in_proof_of_abelian_case}
   \operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\eta( v(n)\operatorname{mod} \mathbb{Z}^d)=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\exp( \zeta_1p_1(n)+\dots+\zeta_Mp_M(n))
\end{equation}
for $\zeta_{1},\dots, \zeta_M\in \mathbb{R}$ which are irrational or equal to $0$ (since $\overline{s_i\Gamma}$ is connected for all $i$). At least one $\zeta_i$ must be nonzero and so (\ref{eq:weyl_criterion_in_proof_of_abelian_case}) is equal to $0$ by Theorem \ref{thm:Boshernitzan_wd}. This concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- theorem-26: body_md -->
\label{thm:sub_linear}
    Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} is true under the added assumptions that $s_1=\cdots=s_M = 1_G$ and $g_{m}(x)\prec x$.
<!-- end theorem-26 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
As in the proof of \cite[Theorem 4.2]{Richter23} we assume that $X  = \overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_{m}^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}$, that $z_{m}\neq 1_G$, and that $G$ is a $d$-step nilpotent group and we proceed by induction on $d$.

    In the base case $d=1$, $G$ is abelian and so we know that the theorem statement holds in this case by Proposition \ref{prop:abelian_case}. So we suppose that $d\geqslant 2$ and that the statement of the theorem is true for any $d-1$-step nilpotent group. Let $L$ be the smallest connected, closed, rational, normal subgroup of $G$ which contains $z_{m}^{\mathbb{R}}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:richter_equivalence}, it suffices to show that 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:richter_equivalence_again}
        \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\varphi(v(n)\Gamma)=0
    \end{equation}
    holds for each central character $(\varphi,\chi)$ such that $\chi$ is nontrivial on $L^{\circ}\cap Z(G)^{\circ}$. To this end, define
    \begin{equation}
        A(\varepsilon) = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\left(\varphi(v(n)z_{m}^{\varepsilon}\Gamma)\cdot \overline{\varphi(v(n)\Gamma)}\right)
    \end{equation}
as in the statement of Theorem \ref{thm:funny_vdc}. To show that (\ref{eq:richter_equivalence_again}) holds we will apply Theorem \ref{thm:funny_vdc} with $\Psi(x_1,\dots, x_m) = \varphi(z_1^{g_1(x_1)}\cdots z_{m}^{g_{m}(x_m)})$ by first showing that the limit in the definition of $A(\varepsilon)$ exists for each $\varepsilon\in \mathbb{R}$ and that for each $\delta>0$ there is a $\xi\in (0,\delta)$ such that $\lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}A(\xi h) = 0$. Once we have shown these two facts, we will have shown that $\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\varphi(v(n)\Gamma)=0$ as desired.

 Fix $\varepsilon\in \mathbb{R}$, put $b=z_{m}$, define $v^{\triangle}(n) = (v(n),v(n))$ for $n\in \mathbb{N}$, and define $\Phi_{\varepsilon}(g\Gamma)=\varphi(g\cdot b^{\varepsilon}\Gamma)\cdot \overline{\varphi(g\Gamma)}$ for all $g\in X$. Then 
\begin{equation}
    A(\varepsilon)  = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\Phi_{\varepsilon}(v(n)\Gamma).
\end{equation}
Additionally, let $\widehat{G} = G/Z(G)$, let $\sigma:G\rightarrow \widehat{G}$ be the quotient map, and put $\widehat{\Gamma} = \sigma(\Gamma)$, $\widehat{v}(n) = \sigma(v(n))$, and $\widehat{X} = \widehat{G}/\widehat{\Gamma} = \overline{\sigma(z_1)^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots \sigma(z_{m})^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}$. Note that $\Phi_{\varepsilon}(yx\Gamma) = \Phi(x\Gamma)$ for all $y\in Z(G)$, and so the function $\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}:\widehat{X}\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ defined by  $\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(g\widehat{\Gamma}) = {\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(g\Gamma)$ is well defined.


Note that $\widehat{G}$ is a $d-1$-step nilpotent group. By the induction hypothesis, we have that 
\begin{align*}
    A(\varepsilon)  = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\Phi_{\varepsilon}(v(n)\Gamma) = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(v(n)\widehat{\Gamma})= \int_{\widehat{X}}\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}d\mu_{\widehat{X}} = \int_X{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}d\mu_{{X}}.
\end{align*}
Define $\Phi:X\times_L X\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ by $\Phi((g_1,g_2)\Gamma\times_L\Gamma) =\varphi(g_1\Gamma)\overline{\varphi(g_2\Gamma)}$, so that $\Phi$ is a continuous with $\Phi((b^{\varepsilon}g,g)\Gamma\times_L\Gamma) = \Phi_{\varepsilon}(g\Gamma )$ for all $g\in G$. Then
\begin{align*}
\int \Phi_{\varepsilon}d\mu_{{X}} = \int_{(x,x)\in X^{\triangle}} \Phi((b^{\varepsilon}x,x)\Gamma\times_L\Gamma)d\mu_{{X^{\triangle}}}.
\end{align*}
Lastly, from Lemma \ref{lem:wd_in_diagonal}, for each $\delta>0$ there exists a value of $\xi\in (0,\delta)$ such that the sequence of subnilmanifolds $(b^{\xi n}, 1_G)X^{\triangle}))_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed in $X\times_L X$, which shows that
\begin{equation}
    \lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}A(\xi h) = \lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H} \int_{(x,x)\in X^{\triangle}} \Phi((b^{\xi h}x,x)\Gamma\times_L\Gamma)d\mu_{{X^{\triangle}}} = \int_{X\times_L X}\Phi d\mu_{X\times_L X}.
\end{equation}
Since $\int\Phi d\mu_{X\times_L X}=0$ by \cite[Claim 1 p. 449]{Richter23}, we have completed the proof of the induction step and so we are done.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
As in \cite[p. 453]{Richter23}, we assume without loss of generality that $X = \overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_{m}^{\mathbb{R}}\cdot s_1^{\mathbb{Z}}\cdots s_M^{\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma}$ and that $z_i\neq 1_G$ for all $i$. Indeed, one can replace $G$ with the smallest closed, rational subgroup of $G$ containing $z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_{m}^{\mathbb{R}}\cdot s_1^{\mathbb{Z}}\cdots s_M^{\mathbb{Z}}$ if necessary. 
 
 Let $\mathscr{I}$ be the set of all $i$ such that $\deg(g_i)\geqslant 2$ and $\mathscr{J}$ the set of all $j$ such that $s_j\neq 1_{G}$. If $\mathscr{I}\cup \mathscr{J}=\varnothing$ then we can apply Theorem \ref{thm:sub_linear}, so suppose that $\mathscr{I}\cup \mathscr{J}\neq \varnothing$. Let $L$ be the smallest closed, connected, rational, normal subgroup of $G$ which contains $z_i^{\mathbb{R}}$ for all $i\in \mathscr{I}$ and $s_{j}^{\mathbb{Z}}$ for all $j=1,\dots, M$. Richter shows that $L^{\circ}\cap Z(G)^{\circ}$ is nontrivial \cite[p. 454]{Richter23} and so by Lemma \ref{lem:richter_equivalence} it suffices to show that 
 \begin{equation}\label{eq:generalized_G_phi}
     \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\varphi(v(n)\Gamma)=0
 \end{equation}
 for each central character $(\varphi,\chi)$ such that $\chi$ is nontrival on $L^{\circ}\cap Z(G)^{\circ}$. Define 
 $$
 A(h) = \lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W(\varphi(v(n+h)\Gamma)\cdot \overline{\varphi(v(n)\Gamma)}).
 $$
  We will prove that
 \begin{equation}
\lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}A(h)=0
 \end{equation}
 so that (\ref{eq:generalized_G_phi}) follows by Lemma \ref{lem:vdc} (or by {\cite[Theorem A.8]{Richter23}}).


 


Now we appeal to following facts proven by Richter \cite[pp. 453-466]{Richter23}.
 \begin{itemize}
    \item There is a continuous function $\Phi:X\times_L X\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ defined by 
    \begin{equation}
        \Phi((g_1,g_2)\Gamma\times_L \Gamma) = \varphi(g_1\gamma)\cdot \overline{\varphi(g_2\Gamma)} \quad \text{ for all} (g_1,g_2)\in G\times_L G.
    \end{equation}
     \item Let $Z(G)^{\triangle} = \{(g,g):g\in Z(G)\}$ and let $\sigma: G\times_L G\rightarrow G\times_L G/Z(G)^{\triangle}$ be the quotient map. Define $\widehat{G} = \sigma(G)$, $\widehat{\Gamma}= \sigma(\Gamma)$, $\widehat{X}=\widehat{G}/\widehat{\Gamma}$. Then $\Phi$ induces a continuous function $\widehat{\Phi}:\widehat{X}\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ such that 
         \begin{equation}
        \Phi((g_1,g_2)\Gamma\times_L \Gamma) = \widehat{\Phi}(\sigma(g_1,g_2)\widehat{\Gamma}) \quad \text{ for all }~ (g_1,g_2)\in G\times_L G.
        \end{equation}
    \item $\int \widehat{\Phi}~d\mu_{\widehat{X}}=0$.
    \item There exists a sequence of subnilmanifolds of $\widehat{X}$, $(\widehat{Y}_h)_{h\in \mathbb{N}}$ such that $A(h) = \int \widehat{\Phi}~d\mu_{\widehat{Y}_h}$ for all $h\in \mathbb{N}$.
    \item There exists a sequence $(w(h,\mathbf{n}, \mathbf{l}))_{h\in \mathbb{N}, \mathbf{n}\in \mathbb{N}^{m},\mathbf{l}\in \mathbb{N}^{M}}$ in $\widehat{X}$ which is well distributed in $\widehat{X}$ and for each fixed $h\in \mathbb{N}$, the sequence $(w(h,\mathbf{n}, \mathbf{l}))_{\mathbf{n}\in \mathbb{N}^{m},\mathbf{l}\in \mathbb{N}^{M}}$ is uniformly distributed in $\widehat{Y}_h$.
 \end{itemize}
 
Now we conclude the proof by noting that 
\begin{align*}
    &\lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}A(h)= \lim_{H\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}\int \widehat{\Phi}~d\mu_{\widehat{Y}_h}\\ =& \lim_{H\to\infty}\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}\left(\frac{1}{N^{m+M}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in\{1,\dots, N\}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\{1,\dots, N\}^M}\widehat{\Phi}(w(h,\mathbf{n}, \mathbf{l}))\right).
\end{align*}
 
 Since $(w(h,\mathbf{n}, \mathbf{l}))_{h\in \mathbb{N},\mathbf{n}\in \mathbb{N}^{m},\mathbf{l}\in \mathbb{N}^{M}}$ is well distributed in $\widehat{X}$, observe that Remark \ref{remark:wd_means_W_ud} holds with 
 $$
 \alpha_{H,N_1,\dots, N_{m+M},h,n_1,\dots, n_{m+M}} = \frac{1}{HN_1\cdots  N_{m+M}}\cdot 1_{h\leqslant H}\cdot 1_{\{n_1\leqslant N_1\}}\cdots 1_{\{n_{m+M}\leqslant N_{m+M}\}}
 $$
 to see that that
 \begin{equation}
     \lim_{H\to\infty}\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{h\leqslant H}\left(\frac{1}{N^{m+M}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in\{1,\dots, N\}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\{1,\dots, N\}^M}\widehat{\Phi}(w(h,\mathbf{n}, \mathbf{l}))\right) = \int \widehat{\Phi}~d\mu_{\widehat{X}}= 0,
 \end{equation}
 as desired.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:richter_A.6}
Let $\mathcal{H}$ be a Hardy field. Suppose that $W,f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\in \mathcal{H}$ such that $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) and $1 \prec \log W(x)\prec x$. Then there exists $m \in \mathbb{N}$, $g_1,\dots, g_m \in \mathcal{H}$, $p_1(t),\dots , p_{\ell}(t) \in \mathbb{R}[t]$, and $(\lambda_{i,j})_{i,j=1}^{\ell,m}\subseteq \mathbb{R}$
with the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}[label = (\arabic*)]
    \item $g_1(t)\prec \cdots \prec g_m(t)$,
    \item for all nonzero $g\in \{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$, there exists $d\in \mathbb{N}$ such that $x^{d-1}\prec g(x)\prec x^d$,
    \item for all $g\in \{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$, either $W$ is compatible with $g$ or there is an $i\in \{1,\dots, m\}$ such that $g=g_i'$,
    \item for all $g\in \{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$ with $\deg(g)\geqslant 2$, $g'\in \{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$,
    \item for all $i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}$,
    $$
    \lim_{t\to\infty}\left|f_i(t)-\sum_{j=1}^m\lambda_{i,j}g_j(t)-p_i(t)\right|=0.
    $$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
\cite[Lemma A.4]{Richter23} says that there exist $(\lambda_{i,j})_{i,j=1}^{\ell,m}\subseteq \mathbb{R}$, $p_1(t),\dots, p_{\ell}(t)\in \mathbb{R}[t]$, and $g_1,\dots, g_m \in \text{Span}\{f^{(k)}_i:i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}, k\in \{0,1,2,\dots\}\}$ such that the following hold
     \begin{itemize}
         \item $g_1\prec \dots \prec g_m$,
         \item all $g\in \{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$, either $g=0$ or $\deg(g)\geqslant 1$ and $x^{\deg(g)-1}\prec g(x)\prec x^{\deg(g)}$,
         \item for all $g\in \{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$, if $\deg(g)\geqslant 2$ then $g' = g_j$ for some $j\in \{1,\dots, m\}$, and if $1\leqslant \deg(g)\leqslant \deg(g_m)-1$ then $g_j'=g$ for some $j\in\{1,\dots, m\}$,
         \item 
         for each $i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:A.6}
    \lim_{t\to\infty}\left|f_i(t)-\sum_{j=1}^m\lambda_{i,j}g_j(t)-p_i(t)\right|=0.
    \end{equation}
     \end{itemize}
     The only thing to prove is that this collection $\{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$ satisfies condition (3) in the statement of Lemma \ref{lem:richter_A.6}. To this end, pick $j\in \{1,\dots, m\}$ such that there is no $i\in \{1,\dots, m\}$ with $g_i'=g_j$. We will show that $W$ is compatible with $g_j$. First note that $\deg(g_j) = \deg(g_m) = \max\{\deg(f_i):i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}\}$, since otherwise $\{g_1,\dots, g_m\}$ would contain an anti-derivative of $g_j$. From the fact that $\deg(g_j) =\max\{\deg(f_i):i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}\}$ and $g_j\in \text{Span}\{f_i^{(k)}:i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\},k\in \{0,1,2,\dots\}\}$, there is an $f \in \text{Span}\{f_i:i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}\} $ with $g_j(x) = f(x)\cdot (1+o_{x\to\infty}(1))$. Then $W$ is compatible with $g_j$ because $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}).
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Let $m \in \mathbb{N}$, let $g_1,\dots, g_m \in \mathcal{H}$, let $p_1(t),\dots , p_{\ell}(t) \in \mathbb{R}[t]$, and let $(\lambda_{i,j})_{i,j=1}^{\ell,m}\subseteq \mathbb{R}$ be given by Lemma \ref{lem:richter_A.6}. As in \cite[Section 3]{Richter23}, consider the following definitions. 

    Let $M\in \mathbb{N}$ and let $(c_{i,j})_{i,j=1}^{\ell,M}$ be real numbers such that 
    \begin{equation*}
        p_i(n)=\sum_{j=0}^Mc_{i,j}\binom{n}{j}.
    \end{equation*}
    Define
    \begin{equation*}
        u_j = \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}a_i^{\lambda_{i,j}}\text{ for } j\in \{1,\dots, m\} \text{ and } e_j= \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}a_i^{c_{i,j}}\text{ for } j\in \{1,\dots, M\}.
    \end{equation*}
    Then by (\ref{eq:A.6})
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:change_of_variables_for_nilsequence}
        u_1^{g_1(n)}\cdots u_m^{g_m(n)}e_1^{\binom{n}{1}}\cdots e_M^{\binom{n}{M}} = a_1^{f_1(n)+o_{n\to\infty}(1)}\cdots a_{\ell}^{f_{\ell}(n)+o_{n\to\infty}(1)},
    \end{equation}
    and so it suffices to show that the conclusions of Theorem \ref{thm_generalized_richter_D} hold for the sequence $(w(n)\Gamma)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$, where
    \begin{equation}
        w(n)= u_1^{g_1(n)}\cdots u_m^{g_m(n)}e_1^{\binom{n}{1}}\cdots e_M^{\binom{n}{M}}.
    \end{equation}

    It is shown in \cite[pg. 441]{Richter23} that there exists a $q\in \mathbb{N}$ such that $\overline{e_j^{q\mathbb{Z}+r}\Gamma}$ is a connected sub-nilmanifold of $X$ for all $j\in \{1,\dots, M\}$ and all $r\in \{0,\dots, q-1\}$. Define $p_{j,r}(n) = \frac{1}{q}\left(\binom{qn+r}{j}-\binom{r}{j}\right)$, $s_j = e_j^q$, $c_r = e_1^{\binom{r}{1}}\cdots e_M^{\binom{r}{M}}$, $h_{i,r}(t) = q^{-\deg(g_i)}g_i(qt+r)$, $z_i = u_i^{q^{\deg(g_i)}}$ and put 
    $$
    w_r(n)=z_1^{h_{1,r}(n)}\cdots z_m^{h_{m,r}(n)}\cdot s_1^{p_{1,r}(n)}\cdots s_M^{p_{M,r}(n)}
    $$
    for $j\in \{1,\dots, M\}$, $i\in \{1,\dots, m\}$, $r\in \{0,\dots, q-1\}$. Then 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:w_r_equation}
    w(qn+r) = w_r(n)c_r
    \end{equation}
    for all $r\in \{0,\dots, q-1\}$. For each $r\in \{0,\dots, q-1\}$, the polynomials $p_{1,r},\dots, p_{M,r}$ satisfy conditions (1) and (2) of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G}, the functions $h_{1,r},\dots, h_{m,r}$ belong to $\mathcal{H}$ and satisfy condition (3) of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} and satisfy condition (5) of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} by the chain rule. The following claim shows that condition (4) of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} is also satisfied.
    
    
    \textbf{Claim:} Let $r\in \{0,1,2,\dots, q-1\}$. Let $\eta:X\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ be a horizontal character which is nontrivial on $\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_m^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}$. Then $\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\eta\left(w_r(n)
    \Gamma\right)=0.$
    
    To see why this claim is true, note that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:claim_eq_2}
    \eta(w_r(n)\Gamma) = \eta(a_1^{f_1(qn+r)}\cdots a_{\ell}^{f_{\ell}(qn+r)}c_r^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot (1+o_{n\to\infty}(1))
    \end{equation}
     by (\ref{eq:change_of_variables_for_nilsequence}), (\ref{eq:w_r_equation}), and the continuity of $\eta$.
    Put $\eta(a_i\Gamma) = \alpha_i$ for $i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}$ and $\eta(c_r^{-1}\Gamma) = \rho$. Then the right-hand side of (\ref{eq:claim_eq_2}) becomes
    $$
    \exp(2\pi i (\alpha_1 f_1(qn+r)+\cdots +\alpha_{\ell}f_{\ell}(qn+r)))\cdot \rho\cdot (1+o_{n\to\infty}(1))
    $$
    $\eta$ is nontrivial on $\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_m^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}\subseteq \overline{a_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots a_{\ell}^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}$ and so there exists $i\in \{1,\dots, \ell\}$ such that $\alpha_i\neq 0$. There exists a nonzero $f\in \text{Span}\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ such that $\alpha_1f_1(qn+r)+\cdots +\alpha_{\ell}f_{\ell}(qn+r) = f(qn+r)\cdot (1+o_{n\to\infty}(1))$. Hence
    \begin{align}\label{eq:w_r_and_f}
   \eta(w_r(n)\Gamma) 
   = \exp(f(qn+r)\cdot (1+o_{n\to\infty}(1)))\cdot \rho \cdot (1+o_{n\to\infty}(1)).
    \end{align}
    If $f$ is bounded then $\lim_{n\to\infty}f(qn+r)$ exists and so the right-hand side of (\ref{eq:w_r_and_f}) would tend to a finite limit as $n\to\infty$, which is impossible since the left-hand side of (\ref{eq:w_r_and_f}) does not tend to a limit as $n\to\infty$, because each of the functions $h_{1,r},\dots, h_{m,r},p_{1,r},\dots, p_{M,r}$ tend to $\infty$ and have different growth rates. Therefore $f$ is unbounded and so the sequence $(f(qn+r))_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is u.d. mod 1 with respect to $W$-averages by Theorem \ref{thm:W_ud}, because $\{f_1,\dots, f_{\ell}\}$ satisfies property (\ref{eq:W_compatible}) and $\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{f^{(d)}(qx+r)}{f^{(d)}(x)}\in (0,\infty)$ for $d=\deg^*(f)$. It follows that 
    $$
    \lim_{N\to\infty} \eta(w_r(n)\Gamma) 
   = \lim_{N\to\infty}\exp(f(qn+r))\cdot \rho =0,
    $$
    which proves the claim and shows that condition \ref{condition_(4)} of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} holds.
   

    Each of the conditions of Theorem \ref{thm:generalized_G} hold, so for each $r\in \{0,1\dots, q-1\}$ the sequence $(w_r(n)c_r\Gamma)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is uniformly distributed in the subnilmanifold
    \begin{align*}
       Y_r:= &\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_m^{\mathbb{R}}s_1^{\mathbb{Z}}\cdots s_{M}^{\mathbb{Z}}c_r\Gamma}\\
       =&c_r\cdot \overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_m^{\mathbb{R}}s_1^{\mathbb{Z}}\cdots s_{M}^{\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma}.
    \end{align*}
    Define $Z  =\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_m^{\mathbb{R}}e_1^{\mathbb{Z}}\cdots e_{M}^{\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma}$ so that $c_r\in Z$ and $Y_r\subseteq Z$ for all $r\in \{0,\dots, r-1\}$. Each $Y_r$ is connected because of our assumption that $\overline{e^{q\mathbb{Z}+r}\Gamma}$ is connected for each $r\in \{0,1,\dots, q-1\}$. Each left translation of $Y_0$ is either disjoint from $Y_0$ or equal to $Y_0$, and we observe that 
    $$
    \bigcup_{0\leqslant r_1,\dots, r_M\leqslant q-1}e_1^{r_{1}}\cdots e_{M}^{r_M}Y_0 = Z.
    $$
From this we conclude that $Y_0$ is a connected component of $Z$. 
    Define 
    $$
    \tilde{H} = \{g\in G: gZ=Z\}, \quad H = \tilde{H}^{\circ}
    $$
    so that $Z = \tilde{H}\Gamma$ and $Y_0 = H\Gamma$. Let $x_r = c_r\gamma$ so that $Y_r = Hx_r$ for all $r$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
Let $r\in \{0,1,2,\dots, q-1\}$. Let $\eta:X\rightarrow \mathbb{C}$ be a horizontal character which is nontrivial on $\overline{z_1^{\mathbb{R}}\cdots z_m^{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma}$. Then $\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{n\leqslant N}^W\eta\left(w_r(n)
    \Gamma\right)=0.$
<!-- end head-2 -->
